    70	
    71	--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    72	
    73	:code:`sepal_ui` is a lib designed to create elegant python based dashboard in the `SEPAL environment <https://sepal.io/>`__. It is designed on top of the amazing `ipyvuetify <https://ipyvuetify.readthedocs.io/en/latest/introduction.html>`_ library and will help developer to easily create interface for their workflows.
    74	By using this libraries, you'll ensure a robust and unified interface for your scripts and a easy and complete integration into the SEPAL dashboard of application.
    75	
    76	The full documentation is available `here <https://sepal-ui.readthedocs.io/en/latest/>`__ and demo apps can be launched on Heroku following these links:
    77	
    78	-   `Map style application <https://sepal-map-app.herokuapp.com>`__
    79	-   `Panel style application <https://sepal-panel-app.herokuapp.com>`__
    80	
    81	We are happy to receive feedback and we welcome any kind of contribution.
    82	
    83	+-----------+-------------+
    84	| |map-app| | |panel-app| |
    85	+-----------+-------------+
    86	
    87	.. |map-app| image:: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/12rambau/sepal_ui/main/docs/source/_image/demo-map-app.png
    88	    :width: 100%
    89	
    90	.. |panel-app| image:: https://raw.githubusercontent.com/12rambau/sepal_ui/main/docs/source/_image/demo-panel-app.png
    91	    :width: 100%
    92	
    93	Contribute
    94	----------
    95	
    96	If you want to contribute you can fork the project in you own repository and then use it.
    97	If you consider working with us, please follow the `contributing guidelines <CONTRIBUTING.rst>`__.
    98	
    99	Meet our `contributor <AUTHORS.rst>`__.

**Final Answer**
\boxed{2018!}
</think>To solve the problem, we need to determine the maximum number of ways to pick stones from different boxes with different numbers such that their total mass is at least 1 kilogram.

### Problem Restatement
Each of the 2019 boxes contains stones numbered from 1 to 2019, and the total mass of all stones is 1 kilogram. We need to find the maximum number of ways to pick one stone from each box such that all selected stones have distinct numbers and their total mass is at least 1 kilogram.

### Solution

1. **Understanding the Setup:**
   - Each box contains 2019 stones, each numbered from 1 to 2019.
   - The total mass of all stones is 1 kilogram.
   - We need to pick one stone from each box such that all selected stones have distinct numbers. This is equivalent to selecting a permutation of the numbers 1 to 2019.

2. **Optimal Mass Distribution Strategy:**
   - To maximize the number of valid permutations with a total mass of at least 1 kilogram, we should concentrate the entire mass of 1 kilogram into a single stone.
   - For example, place the entire mass of 1 kilogram on one specific stone (e.g., the stone numbered 1 in box 1) and make all other stones' masses zero.

3. **Counting Valid Permutations:**
   - If the mass of 1 kilogram is placed on a single stone (say, the stone numbered 1 in box 1), any permutation that includes this stone will have a total mass of exactly 1 kilogram.
   - The number of such permutations is the number of ways to permute the remaining 2018 numbers in the remaining 2018 boxes, which is \(2018!\).

4. **Conclusion:**
   - By concentrating the mass into one stone, we ensure that the number of valid permutations is maximized, leading to the result \(2018!\).

Thus, the maximum number of ways \(k\) is:

\[
\boxed{2018!}

   116

   123	    @observe("v_model")
   124	    def check_date(self, change: dict) -> None:
   125	        """
   126	        Check if the data is formatted date.
   127	
   128	        A method to check if the value of the set v_model is a correctly formated date
   129	        Reset the widget and display an error if it's not the case.
   130	        """
   131	        self.date_text.error_messages = None
   132	
   133	        # exit immediately if nothing is set
   134	        if not change["new"]:
   135	            return
   136	
   137	        # change the error status
   138	        if not self.is_valid_date(change["new"]):
   139	            msg = self.date_text.hint
   140	            self.date_text.error_messages = msg
   141	
   142	        return
   143	
   144	    @observe("v_model")
   145	    def close_menu(self, change: dict) -> None:
   146	        """A method to close the menu of the datepicker programatically."""
   147	        # set the visibility
   148	        self.menu.v_model = False
   149	
   150	        return
   151	
   152	    @observe("disabled")
   153	    def disable(self, change: dict) -> None:
   154	        """A method to disabled the appropriate components in the datipkcer object."""
   155	        self.menu.v_slots[0]["children"].disabled = self.disabled
   156	
   157	        return
   158	
   159	    @staticmethod
   160	    def is_valid_date(date: str) -> bool:
   161	        """
   162	        Check if the date is provided using the date format required for the widget.
   163	
   164	        Args:
   165	            date: the date to test in YYYY-MM-DD format
   166	
   167	        Returns:
   168	            The validity of the date with respect to the datepicker format
   169	        """
   170	        valid = True
   171	        try:
   172	            datetime.strptime(date, "%Y-%m-%d")
   173	        except (ValueError, TypeError):
   174	            valid = False
   175	
   176	        return valid

Okay, so I need to figure out how many polynomials P(x) there are with coefficients in {0, 1, 2, 3} such that P(2) = n, where n is a natural number. Hmm, let's start by understanding what the problem is asking.

First, a polynomial with coefficients in {0, 1, 2, 3} means that each coefficient (the numbers in front of the variables) can be 0, 1, 2, or 3. For example, a polynomial like P(x) = 3x^2 + 2x + 1 would qualify because all coefficients are in the allowed set. The question is, given a specific natural number n, how many different such polynomials evaluate to n when we substitute x = 2. 

So, essentially, if I plug x = 2 into such a polynomial, the result is n, and I need to count how many different polynomials (with the given coefficients) can produce that n. Let's break this down.

Let me consider what P(2) represents. If the polynomial is P(x) = a_k x^k + a_{k-1} x^{k-1} + ... + a_1 x + a_0, then P(2) = a_k * 2^k + a_{k-1} * 2^{k-1} + ... + a_1 * 2 + a_0. Each coefficient a_i is in {0, 1, 2, 3}. Therefore, the problem is equivalent to finding the number of sequences (a_0, a_1, ..., a_k) where each a_i is 0, 1, 2, or 3, such that the sum a_0 + a_1*2 + a_2*2^2 + ... + a_k*2^k equals n.

So for each n, there's a maximum degree needed. For example, if n is 5, then the highest power of 2 needed would be 4 (which is 2^2), since 2^3=8 is larger than 5. In standard base 2, each digit is 0 or 1, and each position represents a power of 2. Here, each coefficient can be 0,1,2,3, but they are multiplied by powers of 2. This seems similar to a number representation in base 4, but in base 4, each digit is 0-3 and the positions represent powers of 4. Here, the positions are powers of 2. So perhaps this is a different kind of number system.

Since the coefficients can be up to 3, each term can contribute up to 3*2^k.

   319	    def reset(self, *args) -> Self:
   320	        """
   321	        Clear the File selection and move to the root folder.
   322	        """
   323	        # note: The args arguments are useless here but need to be kept so that
   324	        # the function is natively compatible with the clear btn
   325	
   326	        # do nothing if nothing is set to avoids extremelly long waiting

   338	    def select_file(self, path: Union[str, Path]) -> Self:
   339	        """
   340	        Manually select a file from it's path. No verification on the extension is performed.
   341	
   342	        Args:
   343	            path: the path to the file
   344	        """
   345	        # cast to Path
   346	        path = Path(path)
   347	
   348	        # test file existence
   349	        if not path.is_file():
   350	            raise Exception(f"{path} is not a file")
   351	
   352	        # set the menu to the folder of the file
   353	        self._on_file_select({"new": path.parent})
   354	
   355	        # select the appropriate file
   356	        self._on_file_select({"new": path})
   357	
   358	        return self
   359	
   360	    def _on_file_select(self, change: dict) -> Self:
   361	        """
   362	        Dispatch the behavior between file selection and folder change.
   363	        """
   364	        if not change["new"]:
   365	            return self
   366	
   367	        new_value = Path(change["new"])
   368	
   369	        if new_value.is_dir():
   370	            self.folder = new_value
   371	            self._change_folder()
   372	
   373	        elif new_value.is_file():
   374	            self.file = str(new_value)
   375	
   376	        return self

- a_1=0: then a_2=1 (since n''=1, which is a_2=1)
- a_1=2: then a_2=0 (since n''=0)

So this recursion seems to work. Therefore, the approach is:

f(n) = sum_{a=0,1,2,3} [if (n - a) is non-negative and even, then f((n - a)/2) else 0]

With the base case f(0) =1.

c_{2,0} + c_{1,1} + carry_1 = d_2 + 2*carry_2

Here, carry_1 is from the previous step (j=1). Depending on the value of carry_1, which can be 0 or 1, we have different possibilities.

This seems to suggest that the number of ways depends on the carries and the digits of n. Each time, the number of possibilities is multiplied by the number of possible combinations for the current bit and carry.

But how can we model this? It looks like a finite automaton where the state is the carry, and transitions depend on the current digit and the previous carry.

Let’s define a dynamic programming table where dp[j][c] represents the number of ways to reach the j-th bit with a carry of c (c can be 0 or 1).

The base case is dp[0][0] =1 (before processing any bits, carry is 0), and dp[0][1] =0.

Then, for each bit j from 0 to m, and for each possible carry c (0 or 1), we compute the transitions.

But since we need to process all bits, and the carry can propagate, we need to process the bits in order. However, this is getting complicated. Let's try to formalize this.

Suppose we have the binary digits of n as b_0, b_1, ..., b_m. We need to compute the number of sequences (c_{0,0}, c_{0,1}, c_{1,0}, c_{1,1}, ..., c_{m,0}, c_{m,1}) such that:

1. For each i, c_{i,0}, c_{i,1} ∈ {0,1} (since a_i = c_{i,0} + 2*c_{i,1} and a_i ≤3, but since c_{i,0} and c_{i,1} are bits, a_i can be 0,1,2,3).

2. For each bit j in the binary representation of n, the equation:

c_{j,0} + c_{j-1,1} + carry_{j-1} = b_j + 2*carry_j.

But wait, this seems too vague. Let's reconsider.

Alternatively, let's model the problem as a finite automaton where each state is the carry, and transitions depend on the current bit and the next.

Let's denote that for each bit position j, after processing up to j, we have a carry out of carry_{j}.

The number of ways to have carry_{j}=c is equal to the sum over all possible previous carry_{j-1} and possible c_{j,0} and c_{j-1,1} that satisfy the equation.

Specifically:

At bit j, the total contribution is c_{j,0} (from a_j) and c_{j-1,1} (from a_{j-1}), plus the incoming carry carry_{j-1}. This sum must equal b_j + 2*carry_j.

Therefore:

c_{j,0} + c_{j-1,1} + carry_{j-1} = b_j + 2*carry_j.

Our variables are c_{j,0}, c_{j-1,1}, carry_j, given carry_{j-1}.

The possible values are c_{j,0} ∈ {0,1}, c_{j-1,1} ∈ {0,1}, carry_j ∈ {0,1}.

But c_{j-1,1} is part of a_{j-1}, which is determined when processing j-1. Wait, but this seems recursive.

Perhaps we need to consider that when processing bit j, we need to determine c_{j,0} and c_{j-1,1}, given the previous carry and producing the next carry.

But this is getting quite involved. Let's attempt to write recurrence relations for the dynamic programming.

Let’s define dp[j][c] as the number of ways to process bits up to j with a carry c. Starting from j=0.

For each j, we can compute dp[j+1][new_c] based on dp[j][prev_c].

The recurrence would be:

For each possible previous carry prev_c (0 or 1), and for each possible c_{j,0} and c_{j,1} (each 0 or1), we have:

sum_{prev_c} dp[j][prev_c] * number of ways to choose c_{j,0} and c_{j,1} such that:

c_{j,0} + c_{j,1}'s contribution plus prev_c equals b_j + 2*new_c.

But this is still vague. Let's try to model it step by step.

At each bit j:

The equation to satisfy is:

c_{j,0} + c_{j-1,1} + prev_c = b_j + 2*new_c.

But c_{j-1,1} is part of a_{j-1}, which was determined when processing j-1. Wait, but when processing j, c_{j-1,1} is already determined. Wait, no. If we are building up the coefficients sequentially, then when processing bit j, we need to determine c_{j,0} and c_{j,1}, which affect bit j and j+1.

This is getting too tangled. Let's look for a different approach. Maybe instead of thinking about carries and bits, use the original recursive formula and find a pattern.

Let’s compute f(n) for small n and see if we can find a pattern.

Base case: f(0)=1.

For n=1:

Possible a_0:1,3 (but 3>1, so only a_0=1. Then (1-1)/2=0. So f(1)=f(0)=1.

For n=2:

Possible a_0:0,2. (2-0)/2=1, (2-2)/2=0. So f(2)=f(1)+f(0)=1+1=2.

n=3:

a_0=1,3. (3-1)/2=1, (3-3)/2=0. So f(3)=f(1)+f(0)=1+1=2.

n=7:

n=8:

a_0=0,2. (8-0)/2=4, (8-2)/2=3. So f(8)=f(4)+f(3)=3+2=5.

- 3x (evaluates to 3*2=6)
- x + x^2 (evaluates to 2 +4=6)

If a_0=2: remaining is6-2=4, divided by2=2. So f(2)=2. The remaining 2 can be achieved with a_1=2 (P(x)=2 +2x) or a_1=0 and a_2=1 (P(x)=2 +0x +1x^2). Therefore, two polynomials:

- 2 +2x (2 +4=6)
- 2 + x^2 (2 +4=6)

So total four polynomials, which matches f(6)=4. So the formula works.

Therefore, the number of polynomials is indeed floor(n/2) +1.

But wait, let's think if there's any case where this might fail. Consider n=0: floor(0/2)+1=0+1=1. Correct.

n=100: floor(100/2)+1=50+1=51. That seems plausible.

Thus, the formula is general. Therefore, the answer should be f(n) = floor(n/2) +1. But since the problem states n is a natural number (which starts at 1), for n=1: floor(1/2)=0, so 0+1=1. Correct.

Therefore, the number of such polynomials is floor(n/2) +1, which can also be written as ⌊n/2⌋ +1.

But in mathematical notation, floor(n/2) is equivalent to n//2 in integer division.

Thus, the answer is floor(n/2) +1. But to confirm, let's check n=1. Only one polynomial: P(x)=1. Which matches.

Yes, so the final answer is that the number of polynomials is ⌊n/2⌋ +1.

**Final Answer**
The number of such polynomials is \boxed{\left\lfloor \dfrac{n}{2} \right\rfloor + 1}.
</think>To determine the number of polynomials \( P(x) \) with coefficients in \(\{0, 1, 2, 3\}\) such that \( P(2) = n \) for \( n \in \mathbb{N} \), we can use a recursive approach. The key insight is that each coefficient \( a_i \) in the polynomial \( P(x) = a_0 + a_1 x + a_2 x^2 + \cdots + a_k x^k \) contributes \( a_i \cdot 2^i \) to the value \( P(2) \).

Define \( f(n) \) as the number of such polynomials that evaluate to \( n \) when \( x = 2 \). The recursive relation for \( f(n) \) is given by:
\[ f(n) = \sum_{\substack{a_0 \in \{0, 1, 2, 3\} \\ (n - a_0) \geq 0 \text{ and } (n - a_0) \equiv 0 \pmod{2}}} f\left(\frac{n - a_0}{2}\right) \]
with the base case \( f(0) = 1 \).

To understand this, consider the possible values of \( a_0 \):
- If \( n \) is even, \( a_0 \) can be 0 or 2.
- If \( n \) is odd, \( a_0 \) can be 1 or 3.

For each valid \( a_0 \), the problem reduces to finding the number of polynomials that evaluate to \(\frac{n - a_0}{2}\).

By computing the values of \( f(n) \) for small \( n \), we observe a pattern:
- \( f(0) = 1 \)
- \( f(1) = 1 \)
- \( f(2) = 2 \)
- \( f(3) = 2 \)
- \( f(4) = 3 \)
- \( f(5) = 3 \)
- \( f(6) = 4 \)
- \( f(7) = 4 \)
- \( f(8) = 5 \)

From this pattern, we conjecture that:
\[ f(n) = \left\lfloor \frac{n}{2} \right\rfloor + 1 \]

To verify this, we can use induction:
1. **Base Case**: For \( n = 0 \), \( f(0) = 1 \), which matches \(\left\lfloor \frac{0}{2} \right\rfloor + 1 = 1\).
2. **Inductive Step**: Assume \( f(k) = \left\lfloor \frac{k}{2} \right\rfloor + 1 \) for all \( k < n \). We need to show \( f(n) = \left\lfloor \frac{n}{2} \right\rfloor + 1 \).

For even \( n \):
\[ f(n) = f\left(\frac{n}{2}\right) + f\left(\frac{n-2}{2}\right) \]
Using the inductive hypothesis:
\[ f\left(\frac{n}{2}\right) = \left\lfloor \frac{n/2}{2} \right\rfloor + 1 = \left\lfloor \frac{n}{4} \right\rfloor + 1 \]
\[ f\left(\frac{n-2}{2}\right) = \left\lfloor \frac{(n-2)/2}{2} \right\rfloor + 1 = \left\lfloor \frac{n-2}{4} \right\rfloor + 1 \]
Since \( \left\lfloor \frac{n-2}{4} \right\rfloor = \left\lfloor \frac{n}{4} \right\rfloor - 1 \):
\[ f(n) = \left( \left\lfloor \frac{n}{4} \right\rfloor + 1 \right) + \left( \left\lfloor \frac{n}{4} \right\rfloor \right) + 1 = \left\lfloor \frac{n}{2} \right\rfloor + 1 \]

For odd \( n \):
\[ f(n) = f\left(\frac{n-1}{2}\right) + f\left(\frac{n-3}{2}\right) \]
Using the inductive hypothesis:
\[ f\left(\frac{n-1}{2}\right) = \left\lfloor \frac{(n-1)/2}{2} \right\rfloor + 1 = \left\lfloor \frac{n-1}{4} \right\rfloor + 1 \]
\[ f\left(\frac{n-3}{2}\right) = \left\lfloor \frac{(n-3)/2}{2} \right\rfloor + 1 = \left\lfloor \frac{n-3}{4} \right\rfloor + 1 \]
Since \( \left\lfloor \frac{n-3}{4} \right\rfloor = \left\lfloor \frac{n-1}{4} \right\rfloor - 1 \):
\[ f(n) = \left( \left\lfloor \frac{n-1}{4} \right\rfloor + 1 \right) + \left( \left\lfloor \frac{n-1}{4} \right\rfloor \right) + 1 = \left\lfloor \frac{n}{2} \<#sep#>B'<<0); ...} ， but byte count 和 unique characters 检查可能 not fit.
```

Since \( \left\lfloor \frac{n-3}{4} \right\rfloor = \left\lfloor \frac{n-1}{4} \right\rfloor - 1 \):
\[ f(n) = \left( \left\lfloor \frac{n-1}{4} \right\rfloor + 1 \right) + \left( \left\lfloor \frac{n-1}{4} \right\rfloor \right) + 1 = \left\lfloor \frac{n}{2} \right\rfloor + 1 \]

Thus, by induction, the formula \( f(n) = \left\lfloor \frac{n}{2} \right\rfloor + 1 \) holds for all \( n \in \mathbb{N} \).

Therefore, the number of polynomials \( P(x) \) such that \( P(2) = n \) is:
\[
\boxed{\left\lfloor \dfrac{n}{2} \right\rfloor + 1}
\]<#sep#>human: You are given a string s. You should answer n queries. The i-th query consists of integer k_i and string m_i. The answer for this query is the minimum length of such a string t that t is a substring of s and m_i has at least k_i occurrences as a substring in t.

A substring of a string is a continuous segment of characters of the string.

It is guaranteed that for any two queries the strings m_i from these queries are different.
Input

The first line contains string s (1 ≤ \left | s \right | ≤ 10^{5}).

The second line contains an integer n (1 ≤ n ≤ 10^5).

Each of next n lines contains an integer k_i (1 ≤ k_i ≤ |s|) and a non-empty string m_i — parameters of the query with number i, in this order.

All strings in input consists of lowercase English letters. Sum of length of all strings in input doesn't exceed 10^5. All m_i are distinct.

Output

For each query output the answer for it in a separate line.

If a string m_{i} occurs in s less that k_{i} times, output -1.

Examples

Input

aaaaa
5
3 a
3 aa
2 aaa
3 aaaa
1 aaaaa


Output

3
4
4
-1
5


Input

abbb
7
4 b
1 ab
3 bb
1 abb
2 bbb
1 a
2 abbb


Output

-1
2
-1
3
-1
1
-1

sepal_ui/sepalwidgets/app.py:6<#sep#>For triangle \(ABC\), let \(S = (\sin A)^2 + (\sin B)^2 + (\sin C)^2\). If \(T = \sin A + \sin B + \sin C\), find the maximum value of \(\frac{S}{T}\).

<think>
Okay, so I need to find the maximum value of S over T, where S is the sum of the squares of the sines of the angles in a triangle, and T is the sum of the sines of the angles. Let me think about how to approach this.

First, let's recall that in any triangle, the sum of the angles is π radians (or 180 degrees). So, A + B + C = π. That might come in handy later. Also, the sine of each angle is positive because all angles are between 0 and π.

Now, we need to maximize S/T, where S = sin²A + sin²B + sin²C and T = sinA + sinB + sinC. Hmm, maybe I can use some trigonometric identities or inequalities here.

One idea is to express this in terms of a single variable. Since the sum of the angles is π, maybe we can express two angles in terms of the third. But with three variables, that might get complicated. Alternatively, maybe assuming the triangle is symmetric, like equilateral, which might give a maximum. Let me check that first.

In an equilateral triangle, all angles are π/3. Then sin(π/3) is √3/2. So S would be 3*(√3/2)^2 = 3*(3/4) = 9/4. T would be 3*(√3/2) = (3√3)/2. So S/T would be (9/4)/(3√3/2) = (9/4)*(2/(3√3)) = (18)/(12√3) = (3)/(2√3) = √3/2 ≈ 0.866.

But is this the maximum? Maybe not. Let's think about other cases.

Suppose the triangle is right-angled. Let's say angle C is π/2. Then sin C = 1. Then angles A and B are complementary, so A + B = π/2. Let me assign A = θ, so B = π/2 - θ.

So then S = sin²θ + sin²(π/2 - θ) + 1. Since sin(π/2 - θ) = cosθ, so S = sin²θ + cos²θ + 1 = 1 + 1 = 2. T = sinθ + cosθ + 1. So S/T = 2/(1 + sinθ + cosθ). Now, to maximize this ratio, we need to minimize the denominator, 1 + sinθ + cosθ.

The minimum of sinθ + cosθ is when? Let's recall that sinθ + cosθ = √2 sin(θ + π/4), which has a minimum value of -√2. But since θ is between 0 and π/2, θ + π/4 is between π/4 and 3π/4, where sin is positive. So the minimum of sinθ + cosθ in this interval is 1, achieved at θ=0 or θ=π/2, but θ can't be 0 or π/2 because then the triangle would be degenerate. Wait, actually when θ approaches 0, sinθ approaches 0 and cosθ approaches 1, so sinθ + cosθ approaches 1. Similarly when θ approaches π/2, sinθ approaches 1 and cosθ approaches 0. So the minimum value of sinθ + cosθ is 1 in this interval. Therefore, the denominator 1 + sinθ + cosθ has a minimum of 2. Therefore, S/T is 2/2 = 1. But wait, in the equilateral case, S/T was √3/2 ≈ 0.866, which is less than 1. So in the right-angled case, we get S/T = 1, which is higher. So maybe the right-angled triangle gives a higher value. Hmm, that's interesting.

Wait, but maybe even higher if we take an isosceles triangle with angles approaching something else. Let me check.

Wait, suppose we have a triangle where one angle approaches 0. Let's say angle C approaches 0, so angles A and B approach π/2. Wait, no, because if angle C approaches 0, then A + B approaches π. So they can't both approach π/2. Actually, if angle C approaches 0, then angles A and B approach π/2 + π/2? No, that would be π. Wait, if angle C approaches 0, then angles A and B must approach π/2 each. Wait, no. Wait, if angle C approaches 0, then A + B approaches π, so perhaps A = B = π/2 - ε/2, where ε approaches 0. Then sin A and sin B would approach sin(π/2) = 1. Then S = sin²A + sin²B + sin²C ≈ 1 + 1 + 0 = 2. T = sinA + sinB + sinC ≈ 1 + 1 + 0 = 2. So S/T ≈ 2/2 = 1. So even in this case, S/T approaches 1. So, total number of KMP runtimes would be, for each m_i, O( len(m_i) + s_length ), sum over all queries of ( m_i_len + s_length ) would be O ( (sum m_i_len) + n*s_length ), but since s_length is 1e5, the second term would be n*s_length = 1e5 *1e5 = 1e10. Which is way too big again.

So that's not going to work.

Yes! The suffix automaton can indeed help here. So, let me recall that a suffix automaton built for s can answer for any pattern p, the number of occurrences and their starting positions.

The suffix automaton can be built in O(n) time. Once it is built, for a query string m_i, you can traverse the suffix automaton to see whether it exists, and how many times it occurs. The count can be retrieved, and the starting positions can be found perhaps in linear time relative to the size of the pattern.

Hmm, perhaps using the Aho's Corasick algorithm (Aho-Corasick automaton). 

Aho-Corasick allows multiple pattern matching. The idea is to process all possible patterns at once, by building an automaton for all the patterns. Then, we can run this automaton over the string s, and track for each occurrence where the patterns are found. Since all the patterns (m_i in the queries) are known in advance, but they are given incrementally in the n queries, then they are all known.

However, the problem says that the sum of the m_i's lengths is up to 1e5, so building an Aho-Corasick automaton is feasible.

Because the Aho-Corasick automaton building time is O(total |m_i|), which is 1e5. Then, processing s through the automaton takes O(|s| + number of occurrences) time. Once this is done, for each m_i, we can have a list of all its occurrences in s. 

Thus this could be a viable approach.

So steps:

- Read s.

- Read all the queries (n of them) collecting all the m_i. 

- Because the problem states that all m_i are different, so we can store them in a list.

But first, we process the queries in a batch.

So the steps are:

Read s.

Read n.

Read all n queries, store the parameters (k_i and m_i), and also store a list of all m_i (so for building the Aho-Corasick trie).

Then, build the Ahocorasick automaton for all the m_i.

Then, run the automaton through the s string to collect for each m_i its occurrence positions. 

Once all the occurrence lists are collected, then for each query (k_i, m_i), you can process it as follows:

- Check if the list for m_i has at least k_i elements. If not, output -1.

- Else, compute the minimal length as the minimal over all possible windows of k_i occurrences:

min{ p[end] - p[start] + (L of m_i) }, (but precise formula as before).

So combine:

   338	    def select_file(self, path: Union[str, Path]) -> Self:
   339	        """
   340	        Manually select a file from it's path. No verification on the extension is performed.
   341	
   342	        Args:
   343	            path: the path to the file
   344	        """
   345	        # cast to Path

   347	
   348	        # test file existence
   349	        if not path.is_file():
   350	            raise Exception(f"{path} is not a file")

   352	        # set the menu to the folder of the file
   353	        self._on_file_select({"new": path.parent})
   354

   356	        self._on_file_select({"new": path})
   357

   359	
   360	    def _on_file_select(self, change: dict) -> Self:
   361	        """
   362	        Dispatch the behavior between file selection and folder change.
   363	        """
   364	        if not change["new"]:
   365	            return self
   366	
   367	        new_value = Path(change["new"])
   368	
   369	        if new_value.is_dir():
   370	            self.folder = new_value
   371	            self._change_folder()
   372	
   373	        elif new_value.is_file():
   374	            self.file = str(new_value)
   375	
   376	        return self

Upon some research, the Aho-Corasick algorithm's process is:

1. Build the trie structure with all the patterns.

2. For each node, compute a failure function (fail pointer) which points to the longest possible proper suffix of the current path that is also a node in the trie.

3. Once processing the input text string, for each character, after jumping via transitions and failures to a node which can't proceed, then all the matches are found in the current node and along its fail links.

Thus, this process allows us to collect all the occurrence starting positions for all patterns in all the queries as we traverse s once. 

Therefore, the plan for code is:

- Preprocess all queries, collect all m_i.

- Build Ahocorasick trie, with each node storing its own matches (m_i's with their length).

- Then, process string s character by character, maintaining:

   - the current node in the trie.

   - for each character, after updating the current node via transitions and failures (standard Ahocorasick traversal), we:

      - walk up all the failure links, collecting all the matches stored in the current nodes along the way.

      - For each such match (m_i, L):

         compute start_pos = current_char_index - L +1.

         add this start_pos to m_i's occurrence list.

- Once all occurs are collected per m_i, process each query.

Thus, the total time is:

Building the trie and failure functions: O(total_length_M (sum len m_i)), which is acceptable.

Processing s: O(len(s) * average path length in failure steps), but this is manageable as it's O(<#sep#>new"]:

</think>To find the maximum value of \(\frac{S}{T}\) where \(S = (\sin A)^2 + (\sin B)^2 + (\sin C)^2\) and \(T = \sin A + \sin B + \sin C\) for a triangle \(ABC\), we will consider several cases and use trigonometric identities and limits.

### Step-by-Step Solution:

1. **Equilateral Triangle Case**:
   - For an equilateral triangle where \(A = B = C = \frac{\pi}{3}\):
     \[
     S = 3 \left(\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}\right)^2 = \frac{9}{4}
     \]
     \[
     T = 3 \left(\frac{\sqrt{3}}{2}\right) = \frac{3\sqrt{3}}{2}
     \]
     \[
     \frac{S}{T} = \frac{\frac{9}{4}}{\frac{3\sqrt{3}}{2}} = \frac{\sqrt{3}}{2} \approx 0.866
     \]

2. **Right-Angled Triangle Case**:
   - Consider a right-angled triangle with angles \(\frac{\pi}{2}\), \(\theta\), and \(\frac{\pi}{2} - \theta\):
     \[
     S = 1 + \sin^2 \theta + \cos^2 \theta = 2
     \]
     \[
     T = 1 + \sin \theta + \cos \theta
     \]
     The minimum value of \(1 + \sin \theta + \cos \theta\) is approached as \(\theta\) approaches 0 or \(\frac{\pi}{2}\), making \(T \approx 2\). Therefore:
     \[
     \frac{S}{T} \approx \frac{2}{2} = 1
     \]

3. **Degenerate Triangle Case**:
   - As one angle approaches 0, the triangle becomes degenerate. Suppose \(A = \frac{\pi}{2} - \epsilon\), \(B = \frac{\pi}{2} - \epsilon\), and \(C = 2\epsilon\) (where \(\epsilon\) is a small positive number):
     \[
     S \approx 2
     \]
     \[
     T \approx 2<#sep#> can precompute for each occurrence list of an m_i the array of p[i +k] (for all i where possible), then compute the difference. However, in coding terms, it would still be O(N) per m_i.

Alternative approach:

The minimal length required for k occurrences of m is: the minimal (p_{i+k-1} - p_i) + L.

Since the occurrences are sorted in p array.

Let me think that to find the minimal delta between p_{i+k} and p_i over all i.

We can see that this is exactly equivalent to the sliding window problem, where we have a sorted list and need to find the minimal window of k elements with the smallest last minus first.

The minimal is simply the minimal of (p[i +k-1] - p[i]) over all valid i.

Since the array is sorted.

   - As one angle approaches 0, the triangle becomes degenerate. Suppose \(A = \frac{\pi}{2} - \epsilon\), \(B = \frac{\pi}{2} - \epsilon\), and \(C = 2\epsilon\) (where \(\epsilon\) is a small positive number):

     \]

    37<#sep#>In a mathematical competition with \( n = 10,000 \) contestants, each contestant in sequence takes a fraction of the remaining cake. The first contestant takes \( \frac{1}{n} \) of the cake, the second contestant takes \( \frac{2}{n} \) of the remaining cake, the third contestant takes \( \frac{3}{n} \) of the remaining cake, and so on. Determine which contestant takes the largest piece of cake.

<think>
Okay, so I need to figure out which contestant takes the largest piece of the cake in this competition. Let's see... There are 10,000 contestants, right? Each one takes a fraction of the remaining cake. The first takes 1/10000, the second takes 2/10000 of what's left, and so on. The key here is to find out at which step the piece taken is the largest. 

Hmm, let's start by understanding the process. The cake starts at 1 (assuming the whole cake is 1 unit). The first contestant takes 1/10000 of it, so the remaining cake after the first person is 1 - 1/10000 = 9999/10000. Then the second contestant takes 2/10000 of the remaining cake. So the amount they take is (2/10000)*(9999/10000). Then the remaining cake after the second would be (9999/10000) - (2/10000)*(9999/10000) = (9999/10000)*(1 - 2/10000). 

Wait, so maybe there's a pattern here. After each contestant k, the remaining cake is multiplied by (1 - k/n). So the amount taken by the k-th contestant is (k/n) multiplied by the remaining cake after the (k-1)th contestant. So, the amount taken by contestant k is (k/n) * product_{i=1 to k-1} (1 - i/n). 

I need to find the k that maximizes this value. Let's denote a_k as the amount taken by contestant k. Then:

a_k = (k/n) * product_{i=1}^{k-1} (1 - i/n)

But since n is 10,000, that's a big number. Maybe we can approximate this product using logarithms? Taking logs might turn the product into a sum, which is easier to handle.

So, ln(a_k) = ln(k/n) + sum_{i=1}^{k-1} ln(1 - i/n)

But ln(1 - x) ≈ -x - x^2/2 - x^3/3 - ... for small x. Since n is 10,000, each term i/n is small when i is much less than n. So maybe we can approximate the sum using integrals?

Wait, the sum from i=1 to k-1 of ln(1 - i/n) is approximately the integral from x=0 to x=(k-1)/n of ln(1 - x) dx multiplied by n? Not sure. Let me think. 

Alternatively, for large n, maybe we can model this as a continuous function. Let’s set x = k/n, so x ranges from 0 to 1 as k goes from 1 to n. Then the term becomes x * product_{i=1}^{k-1} (1 - i/n). But product_{i=1}^{k-1} (1 - i/n) is similar to (1 - 1/n)(1 - 2/n)...(1 - (k-1)/n). 

This product is equal to the product_{m=1}^{k-1} (1 - m/n). Let’s consider the product:

prod_{m=1}^{k-1} (1 - m/n) = \frac{(n - 1)(n - 2)...(n - k + 1)}{n^{k - 1}}} = \frac{(n - 1)!}{n^{k - 1} (n - k)!}

But that might not be helpful. Alternatively, using Stirling's approximation for factorials? But that's getting complicated. Maybe there's another way.

Alternatively, taking the logarithm of the product:

sum_{m=1}^{k-1} ln(1 - m/n) ≈ - sum_{m=1}^{k-1} (m/n + m^2/(2n^2) + ... )

Since m/n is small for m up to k-1, maybe when k is much smaller than n, this sum can be approximated as - sum_{m=1}^{k-1} (m/n) = - ( (k-1)k )/(2n )

But wait, if m is up to k-1, and each term is m/n, so sum m=1 to k-1 of m/n is (k-1)k/(2n). So the approximation of ln(a_k) would be ln(k/n) - (k-1)k/(2n). 

But this is an approximation. Let's check. So:

ln(a_k) ≈ ln(k/n) - (k(k - 1))/(2n)

Since n is large, (k(k - 1))/n is small unless k is large. Hmm. Wait, but if k is up to 10,000, and n is 10,000, so when k approaches n, this term would be significant. So maybe this approximation isn't valid for large k.

Alternatively, perhaps using the integral approximation. The sum_{m=1}^{k-1} ln(1 - m/n) ≈ integral_{0}^{k-1} ln(1 - x/n) dx

Let me make substitution: let t = x/n, so x = nt, dx = n dt. Then the integral becomes n * integral_{0}^{(k-1)/n} ln(1 - t) dt.

Compute the integral:

integral ln(1 - t) dt = - (1 - t)(ln(1 - t) - 1) + C

So evaluating from 0 to (k-1)/n:

n [ - (1 - (k-1)/n)(ln(1 - (k-1)/n) - 1) + (1 - 0)(ln(1 - 0) - 1) ]

Simplify:

= n [ - ( (n -k +1)/n ( ln( (n - k +1)/n ) -1 ) ) -1 ) ]

This is getting too complicated. Maybe there's a better way.

Alternatively, let's model the remaining cake after k contestants. Let R_k be the remaining cake after contestant k. Then:

R_k = R_{k-1} * (1 - k/n)

So R_k = product_{i=1}^k (1 - i/n )

Then the amount taken by contestant k is a_k = (k/n) * R_{k - 1}

We need to find the maximum a_k over k = 1 to n.

To find the maximum, we can look at the ratio a_{k+1}/a_k and see when it's greater than 1 (i.e., when a_{k+1} > a_k) and when it's less than 1 (a_{k+1} < a_k). The maximum will occur at the k where the ratio crosses from above 1 to below 1.

Compute a_{k+1}/a_k:

= ( (k+1)/k ) * ( R_k / R_{k-1} )

But R_k / R_{k-1} = (1 - k/n )

So:

We want to find when this ratio is greater than 1, so:

Multiply both sides by k:

(k + 1)(1 - k/n ) > k

Expand left side:

(k + 1) - (k + 1)k/n > k

Subtract k from both sides:

(k + 1) - (k + 1)k/n - k > 0

Simplify:

1 - (k + 1)k/n > 0

So:

1 > (k(k + 1))/n

Therefore, when (k(k +1 )) < n, then a_{k+1}/a_k > 1. So a<#sep#> →1e5. thus, total N would be 1e5 *1e5 which is impossible.

Multiply both sides by k:

(k + 1)(1 - k/n ) > k

Expand left side:

(k + 1) - (k + 1)k/n > k

Subtract k from both sides:

(k + 1) - (k + 1)k/n - k > 0

Simplify:

1 - (k + 1)k/n > 0

So:

1 > (k(k + 1))/n

Therefore, when (k(k +1 )) < n, then a_{k+1}/a_k > 1. So a_{k+1} is larger than a_k.

So, the maximum occurs when (k(k + 1 )) ≈ n. Let's solve for k. Approximately, k^2 ≈ n, so k ≈ sqrt(n). For n = 10,000, sqrt(n) is 100. But let's check.

Wait, k(k+1) = n. Let's approximate k^2 + k - n = 0. Using quadratic formula:

k = [ -1 ± sqrt(1 + 4n) ] / 2. Taking the positive root:

k ≈ [ -1 + sqrt(4n +1) ] /2. For large n, sqrt(4n) = 2 sqrt(n), so:

k ≈ (2 sqrt(n) - 1)/2 ≈ sqrt(n) - 0.5.

So for n = 10,000, sqrt(n) = 100, so k ≈ 99.5. Therefore, the maximum occurs around k=99 or 100.

Wait, but let's verify. Let's compute (k(k +1 )) for k=100: 100*101=10,100. Which is greater than n=10,000.

So when k=99: 99*100=9,900 <10,000. So at k=99, (k(k+1))=9,900 <n. So a_{k+1}/a_k >1, so a_100 > a_99. Similarly, check for k=100: 100*101=10,100 >10,000. So a_{101}/a_100 = (101/100)*(1 -100/10,000)= (1.01)*(0.99)=1.01*0.99=0.9999 <1. So a_101 <a_100. Therefore, a_100 is the maximum.

Therefore, contestant 100 takes the largest piece.

Wait, but let me check again. Let's compute a_100 and a_99 to make sure.

Compute a_99:

a_99 = (99/10000) * product_{i=1}^{98} (1 - i/10000)

Similarly, a_100 = (100/10000) * product_{i=1}^{99} (1 - i/10000) = (100/10000) * product_{i=1}^{99} (1 - i/10000)

But note that product_{i=1}^{99} (1 - i/10000) = product_{i=1}^{98} (1 - i/10000) * (1 - 99/10000)

Therefore, a_100 = (100/10000) * (1 - 99/10000) * product_{i=1}^{98} (1 - i/10000) 

Similarly, a_99 = (99/10000) * product_{i=1}^{98} (1 - i/10000)

So the ratio a_100 / a_99 = (100/99) * (1 - 99/10000) 

Compute this:

(100/99) * (1 - 0.0099) = (100/99) * 0.9901 ≈ (1.010101...) * 0.9901 ≈ 1.0002. 

So approximately 1.0002, which is slightly greater than 1. So a_100 is a bit larger than a_99. Therefore, the maximum is at k=100.

But wait, but according to our earlier calculation, when k=99, (k(k+1))=99*100=9,900 <10,000, so a_{k+1}/a_k >1. Then when k=100, (k(k+1))=100*101=10,100>10,000, so the ratio a_{k+1}/a_k <1. Therefore, the maximum is at k=100. So contestant 100 takes the largest piece.

Wait, but when n=10,000, which contestant is 100? The first contestant is 1, so the 100th contestant. So answer is 100.

But let me confirm with another approach. Let's think in terms of when the amount a_k starts decreasing. Since the ratio a_{k+1}/a_k = ( (k+1)/k ) * (1 - k/n )

Set this equal to 1:

( (k+1)/k )*(1 - k/n ) =1

Solve for k:

(k+1)(1 - k/n ) =k

Multiply out:

k +1 - k(k+1)/n =k

Subtract k from both sides:

1 - k(k+1)/n =0

Therefore:

k(k+1) =n

So solving k(k+1)=10,000.

Approximate solution: k^2 +k -10,000=0

Using quadratic formula:

k = [ -1 ± sqrt(1 +40,000) ] /2 = [ -1 + sqrt(40001) ] /2

sqrt(40001) is approximately 200.0025, since 200^2=40,000, so 200.0025^2=40,000 +2*200*0.0025 + (0.0025)^2=40,000 +1 +0.00000625=40,001.00000625

So sqrt(40001) ≈200.0025

Thus:

k≈ ( -1 +200.0025 )/2 ≈199.0025/2≈99.50125

So approximately 99.5, which suggests that the maximum is between k=99 and k=100. Since k must be integer, we check which of the two gives the maximum. As before, since a_100 is slightly bigger than a_99, then the maximum is at k=100.

Therefore, contestant 100 takes the largest piece.

Another way to confirm: let's compute a_100 and a_99 more precisely. Although the exact computation would involve huge products, maybe we can approximate using logarithms.

Compute ln(a_k) for k=99 and k=100.

ln(a_k) = ln(k/n) + sum_{i=1}^{k-1} ln(1 - i/n )

For k=99:

ln(a_99) = ln(99/10000) + sum_{i=1}^{98} ln(1 - i/10000 )

Similarly for k=100:

ln(a_100) = ln(100/10000) + sum_{i=1}^{99} ln(1 - i/10000 )

The difference between ln(a_100) and ln(a_99) is:

[ ln(0.01) + sum_{i=1}^{99} ln(1 - i/10000 ) ] - [ ln(0.0099) + sum_{i=1}^{98} ln(1 - i/10000 ) ]

= ln(0.01) - ln(0.0099) + ln(1 -99/10000 )

= ln(0.01 /0.0099) + ln(0.9901 )

Calculate:

0.01 /0.0099 ≈1.010101...

ln(1.010101) ≈0.01005

ln(0.9901) ≈-0.00995

So total difference: 0.01005 -0.00995≈0.0001

So ln(a_100) - ln(a_99)≈0.0001, so a_100 /a_99≈e^{0.0001}≈1.0001, which is just over 1, so a_100 is slightly larger than a_99. Hence, the maximum is at k=100.

Therefore, the answer is contestant number 100.

\boxed{100}
</think>To determine which contestant takes the largest piece of cake, we need to analyze the amount of cake taken by each contestant. Let \( n = 10,000 \) be the total number of contestants. The \( k \)-th contestant takes \( \frac{k}{n} \) of the remaining cake. The remaining cake after \( k \) contestants is given by the product:

\[
R_k = \prod_{i=1}^{k} \left(1 - \frac{i}{n}\right)
\]

The amount of cake taken by the \( k \)-th contestant, \( a_k \), is:

\[
a_k = \frac{k}{n} \cdot R_{k-1} = \frac{k}{n} \prod_{i=1}^{k-1} \left(1 - \frac{i}{n}\right)
\]

To find the maximum \( a_k \), we consider the ratio \( \frac{a_{k+1}}{a_k} \):

\[
\frac{a_{k+1}}{a_k} = \frac{\frac{k+1}{n} \prod_{i=1}^{k} \left(1 - \frac{i}{n}\right)}{\frac{k}{n} \prod_{i=1}^{k-1} \left(1 - \frac{i}{n}\right)} = \frac{k+1}{k} \left(1 - \frac{k}{n}\right)
\]

We set this ratio to 1 to find the critical point:

\[
\frac{k+1}{k} \left(1 - \frac{k}{n}\right) = 1
\]

Solving for \( k \):

\[
(k+1) \left(1 - \frac{k}{n}\right) = k \implies k + 1 - \frac{k(k+1)}{n} = k \implies 1 - \frac{k(k+1)}{n} = 0 \implies k(k+1) = n
\]

For \( n = 10,000 \), we solve the quadratic equation \( k(k+1) = 10,000 \):

\[
k^2 + k - 10,000 = 0
\]

Using the quadratic formula \( k = \frac{-b \pm \sqrt{b^2 - 4ac}}{2a} \) with \( a = 1 \), \( b = 1 \), and \( c = -10,000 \):

\[
k = \frac{-1 \pm \sqrt{1 + 40,000}}{2} = \frac{-1 \pm \sqrt{40,001}}{2}
\]

Approximating \( \sqrt{40,001} \approx 200.0025 \):

\[
k \approx \frac{-1 + 200.0025}{2} \approx 99.50125
\]

Since \( k \) must be an integer, we check \( k = 99 \) and \( k = 100 \). The ratio \( \frac{a_{100}}{a_{99}} \) is:

\[
\frac{a_{100}}{a_{99}} = \frac{100/99} \left(1 - \frac{99}{10,000}\right) = \frac{100}{99} \cdot 0.9901 \approx 1.0001
\]

Since \( \frac{a_{100}}{a_{99}} > 1 \), \( a_{100} \) is slightly larger than \( a_{99} \). Therefore, the maximum piece of cake is taken by the 100th contestant.

\[
\boxed{100}
\]<#sep#>human: A competition is being held between two teams: A and B. Team A has N players numbered from 1 to N. Team B has M players numbered from 1 to M.

The competition requires certain number of matches to be played. In each match, one person from each of the teams goes against one another, until one of them is defeated. The loser is then replaced by another player from his/her team. The team that fails to replace its player (means all players have already lost), loses the competition and no more matches are held.

To be more clear, the players in each team line up to play in their order. That means the first match will be always between the player number 1 in team A and the player number 1 in team B. After the first match, if the player number 1 in team A loses, then he/she will be replaced by the player number 2 in his team, i.e, the second match will be between player number 2 in team A and player number 1 in team B (in case team A has more than one player). Otherwise, the second match will be between player number 1 in team A and player number 2 in team B (in case team B has more than one player). And the two teams keep competing based on that rule until one team has all its players lost.

Given the probability of a player beating another, can you find the expected number of matches that will be played?

Input:
The first line of input file contains T, the number of test cases.
Each test case starts with two space-separated integers, N and M, denoting the number of players on team A and team B, respectively.
Each of the next N lines contains M decimal numbers, separated by a single space. The j^th decimal number on i^th line denotes the probability that the player number i of team A shall defeat the player number j of team B. All the decimal numbers only contain up to 2 digits after the decimal point.

Output:
Output exactly T numbers, each denoting the expected number of matches to be held in scenario of the corresponding test case. Print the answer upto 6 digits after decimal point. As, Hacker-Earth uses exact matching, you should ensure to print exactly 6 places.

Constraints:
1 ≤ T ≤ 3
1 ≤ N, M ≤ 1000

SAMPLE INPUT
1
2 2
0.5 0.5
0.5 0.5

SAMPLE OUTPUT
2.500000

Explanation

Possibilities are as follows: 
- 1A def 1B, 1A def 2B: Probability is 0.5 * 0.5 = 0.25 and number of matches is 2.
- 1A def 1B, 2B def 1A, 2B def 2A: Probability is 0.5 * 0.5 * 0.5=0.125 and number of matches is 3
- 1A def 1B, 2B def 1A, 2A def 2B: Probability is 0.5 * 0.5 * 0.5=0.125 and number of matches is 3
- 1B def 1A, 1B def 2A: Probability is 0.5 * 0.5 = 0.25 and number of matches is 2.
- 1B def 1A, 2A def 1B, 2A def 2B: Probability is 0.5 * 0.5 * 0.5=0.125 and number of matches is 3
- 1B def 1A, 2A def 1B, 2B def 2A: Probability is 0.5 * 0.5 * 0.5=0.125 and number of matches is 3
Thus final answer is:

0.25 * 2 + 0.125 * 3 + 0.125 * 3 + 0.25 * 2 + 0.125 * 3 + 0.125 * 3 = 2.5

First, maybe I should express the equation in terms of prime factors. Since 5 and 6 are coefficients, breaking them down into primes might help. So 5 is a prime number, and 6 is 2 times 3. So the equation is 5a³ = 2*3*b⁴. 

I want to find a and b such that when I multiply a³ by 5, it's the same as multiplying 6 by b⁴. Since a and b are natural numbers, their prime factorizations must make the exponents on both sides match for each prime.

Left side: 5 * (2^x * 3^y * 5^z * ...)³ = 5 * 2^(3x) * 3^(3y) * 5^(3z) * ... = 2^(3x) * 3^(3y) * 5^(3z + 1) * ...

Right side: 2*3 * (2^m * 3^n * 5^p * ...)^4 = 2^(1 + 4m) * 3^(1 + 4n) * 5^(4p) * ...

Since the prime factorizations on both sides must be equal, the exponents of each prime must be equal. Let's set up equations for each prime.

Left exponent: 3x
Right exponent: 1 + 4m

Left exponent: 3y
Right exponent: 1 + 4n

Left exponent: 3z +1
Right exponent: 4p

Alternatively, think of the problem as: the expected number of matches E[a][b], where a is the current index of team A (the next player to play from A must be a, because previous ones have been used) and b similarly for B. The idea is that each step contributes one match, so when you are at (a,b), attempting that match, you can transition to:

With probability P (A's a-th player beats B's b-th player), you transition to (a, b+1) and accumulated 1 match. Or with probability 1-P, you transition to (a+1, b), and also add that current match. So the expectation satisfies the recurrence:

E[a][b] = 1 + P * E[a][b+1] + (1-P) * E[a+1][b]

The base case would be: whenever a exceeds N or b exceeds M, then the game is over. Since whenever the next player is beyond the limit, the game has already ended and no more matches. So when in such an ending state, then the contribution is 0.

Wait let's see. Let me clearly note the recurrence:

The expected value from a state (a,b):

E(a, b) = 1 + P_{a,b} * E(a, b+1) + (1-P_{a,b})*E(a+1, b) 

where P_{a,b} is the probability that A's a-th player beats B's b-th player. The "1" comes from the match currently being played (since that's the first step), then the expectation is that plus the next expectation based on the transition.

However, the base cases are when a > N or B's b exceeds M. So for example, if a>N then E(a,b) = 0. Same if b>M. So for anything beyond N or M, it's over.

Wait, but if a>N, then it's impossible to play anymore, so E is zero. Similarly, if b>M, also zero. But if a>N and b>M, then both teams have lost, but logically in the problem statement, the competition stops once one of them has no more players. So actually, in those states E would remain zero. 

Alright, then the DP works.

Because directly computing this with a recursive DP would be O(N*M), and the steps would need memoization. Alternatively, perhaps we can compute this with a bottom-up DP approach, starting from the base cases and building up towards E(1,1).

   319	    def reset(self, *args) -> Self:
   320	        """
   321	        Clear the File selection and move to the root folder.
   322	        """
   323	        # note: The args arguments are useless here but need to be kept so that
   324	        # the function is natively compatible with the clear btn

   326	        # do nothing if nothing is set to avoids extremelly long waiting

   330	            # move to root

   338	    def select_file(self, path: Union[str, Path]) -> Self:
   339	        """
   340	        Manually select a file from it's path. No verification on the extension is performed.
   341	
   342	        Args:
   343	            path: the path to the file
   344	        """
   345	        # cast to Path
   346	        path = Path(path)
   347	
   348	        # test file existence
   349	        if not path.is_file():
   350	            raise Exception(f"{path} is not a file")
   351	
   352	        # set the menu to the folder of the file
   353	        self._on_file_select({"new": path.parent})
   354	
   355	        # select the appropriate file
   356	        self._on_file_select({"new": path})
   357	
   358	        return self
   359	
   360	    def _on_file_select(self, change: dict) -> Self:
   361	        """
   362	        Dispatch the behavior between file selection and folder change.
   363	        """
   364	        if not change["new"]:
   365	            return self
   366	
   367	        new_value = Path(change["new"])
   368	
   369	        if new_value.is_dir():
   370	            self.folder = new_value
   371	            self._change_folder()
   372	
   373	        elif new_value.is_file():
   374	            self.file = str(new_value)
   375	
   376	        return self
   377	
   378	    @sd.switch("indeterminate", on_widgets=["loading"])
   379	    def _change_folder(self) -> None:
   380	        """
   381	        Change the target folder.
   382	        """
   383	        # get the items
   384	        items = self._get_items()
   385	
   386	        # reset files
   387	        # this is reseting the scroll to top without using js scripts
   388	        self.file_list.children[0].children = []
   389	
   390	        # set the new files
   391	        self.file_list.children[0].children = items
   392	
   393	        return
   394

   409	            ]
   410	
   411	        folder_list = []
   412	        file_list = []
   413	
   414	        for el in list_dir:

   417	                icon = self.ICON_STYLE[""]["icon"]
   418	                color = self.ICON_STYLE[""]["color"][v.theme.dark]
   419	            elif el.suffix in self.ICON_STYLE.keys():
   420	                icon = self.ICON_STYLE[el.suffix]["icon"]

Therefore, for the equation to hold, all primes in a must be 2, 3, 5, and all primes in b must be 2, 3, 5. Since primes on one side must match the other.

Furthermore, the exponents:

For prime2: in a³, exponent is 3x, on the right side exponent is 1 +4m.

For prime3: in a³, exponent is 3y, on the right side exponent is1 +4n.

For prime5: on left side, exponent is1 +3z, on right side exponent is4p.

So these equations must hold:

3x =1 +4m

3y=1 +4n

1 +3z=4p

We need to solve these equations for non-negative integers x, y, z, m, n, p.

The minimal solutions for these equations would be:

For the first equation, 3x ≡1 mod4. Solutions for x are 3,7,11,... with corresponding m=2,5,8,...

Similarly, y=3,7,11,... with n=2,5,8,...

For the third equation, 3z ≡-1 mod4 ⇒3z≡3 mod4 ⇒z≡1 mod4. So z=1,5,9,... with p=1,4,7,...

So the minimal solutions are x=3,y=3,z=1, leading to m=2,n=2,p=1.

Therefore, a=2^3*3^3*5^1=8*27*5=1080, b=2^2*3^2*5^1=4*9*5=180. So a+b=1260. That's the minimal.

Alternatively, if we try higher values of x,y,z, the numbers a and b will get larger, so the sum a+b will be larger. So 1260 is indeed the minimal possible sum.

Hence, the answer is \boxed{1260}

   319	    def reset(self, *args) -> Self:
   320	        """
   321	        Clear the File selection and move to the root folder.
   322	        """
   323	        # note: The args arguments are useless here but need to be kept so that
   324	        # the function is natively compatible with the clear btn
   325	
   326	        # do nothing if nothing is set to avoids extremelly long waiting
   327	        # time when multiple fileInput are reset at the same time as in the aoiView
   328	        if self.v_model is not None:
   329	
   330	            # move to root
   331	            self._on_file_select({"new": Path.home()})
   332	
   333	            # remove v_model
   334	            self.v_model = ""
   335	
   336	        return self
   337	
   338	    def select_file(self, path: Union[str, Path]) -> Self:
   339	        """
   340	        Manually select a file from it's path. No verification on the extension is performed.
   341	
   342	        Args:
   343	            path: the path to the file
   344	        """
   345	        # cast to Path
   346	        path = Path(path)
   347	
   348	        # test file existence
   349	        if not path.is_file():
   350	            raise Exception(f"{path} is not a file")
   351	
   352	        # set the menu to the folder of the file
   353	        self._on_file_select({"new": path.parent})
   354	
   355	        # select the appropriate file
   356	        self._on_file_select({"new": path})
   357	
   358	        return self
   359	
   360	    def _on_file_select(self, change: dict) -> Self:
   361	        """
   362	        Dispatch the behavior between file selection and folder change.
   363	        """
   364	        if not change["new"]:
   365	            return self
   366	
   367	        new_value = Path(change["new"])
   368	
   369	        if new_value.is_dir():
   370	            self.folder = new_value
   371	            self._change_folder()
   372	
   373	        elif new_value.is_file():
   374	            self.file = str(new_value)
   375	
   376	        return self
   377

   380	        """
   381	        Change the target folder.
   382	        """
   383	        # get the items
   384	        items = self._get_items()
   385

Read T.

Then, for each test case:

Read N, M.

Read N lines each line has M numbers as float.

prob is a list of lists. So prob[i] is the i-th team A player, and prob[i][j] is the probability against B's player j+1 (when 0-based). So to get A's player i (starting at 1) vs B's j (starting at 1) → prob[i-1][j-1].

Now, for E array:

Initialize with all zeros. But in Python, create a (N+2) x (M+2) array.

Then loops.

Now, code skeleton.

Sample code:

import sys

def main():
    import sys
    input = sys.stdin.read().split()
    idx = 0
    T = int(input[idx]); idx +=1
    for _ in range(T):
        N = int(input[idx]); idx +=1
        M = int(input[idx]); idx +=1
        prob = []
        for i in range(N):
            row = list(map(float, input[idx:idx+M]))
            prob.append(row)
            idx +=M
        # Initialize E as (N+2) x (M+2)
        E = [ [0.0]*(M+2) for _ in range(N+2) ]
        # fill E table
        for a in range(N,0,-1):
            for b in range(M,0,-1):
                p = prob[a-1][b-1]
                term1 = p * E[a][b+1]
                term2 = (1 - p)*E[a+1][b]
                E[a][b] = 1 + term1 + term2
        print("{0:.6f}".format(E[1][1]))
        
if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

   395	    def _get_items(self) -> List[v.ListItem]:
   396	        """
   397	        Create the list of items inside the folder.
   398	
   399	        Returns:
   400	            list of items inside the selected folder
   401	        """
   402	        folder = self.folder
   403	
   404	        list_dir = [el for el in folder.glob("*/") if not el.name.startswith(".")]
   405	
   406	        if self.extentions:
   407	            list_dir = [
   408	                el for el in list_dir if el.is_dir() or el.suffix in self.extentions
   409	            ]
   410	
   411	        folder_list = []
   412	        file_list = []
   413	
   414	        for el in list_dir:
   415	
   416	            if el.is_dir():
   417	                icon = self.ICON_STYLE[""]["icon"]
   418	                color = self.ICON_STYLE[""]["color"][v.theme.dark]
   419	            elif el.suffix in self.ICON_STYLE.keys():
   420	                icon = self.ICON_STYLE[el.suffix]["icon"]
   421	                color = self.ICON_STYLE[el.suffix]["color"][v.theme.dark]
   422	            else:
   423	                icon = self.ICON_STYLE["DEFAULT"]["icon"]
   424	                color = self.ICON_STYLE["DEFAULT"]["color"][v.theme.dark]
   425	
   426	            children = [
   427	                v.ListItemAction(children=[v.Icon(color=color, children=[icon])]),
   428	                v.ListItemContent(
   429	                    children=[v.ListItemTitle(children=[el.stem + el.suffix])]
   430	                ),
   431	            ]
   432	
   433	            if el.is_dir():
   434	                folder_list.append(v.ListItem(value=str(el), children=children))
   435	            else:
   436	                file_size = su.get_file_size(el)
   437	                children.append(
   438	                    v.ListItemActionText(class_="ml-1", children=[file_size])
   439	                )
   440	                file_list.append(v.ListItem(value=str(el), children=children))
   441	
   442	        folder_list = humansorted(folder_list, key=lambda x: x.value)
   443	        file_list = humansorted(file_list, key=lambda x: x.value)
   444	
   445	        parent_item = v.ListItem(
   446	            value=str(folder.parent),
   447	            children=[
   448	                v.ListItemAction(
   449	                    children=[
   450	                        v.Icon(
   451	                            color=self.ICON_STYLE["PARENT"]["color"][v.theme.dark],
   452	                            children=[self.ICON_STYLE["PARENT"]["icon"]],
   453	                        )
   454	                    ]
   455	                ),
   456	                v.ListItemContent(
   457	                    children=[v.ListItemTitle(children=[f".. /{folder.parent.stem}"])]
   458	                ),
   459	            ],
   460	        )
   461	
   462	        folder_list.extend(file_list)
   463	        folder_list.insert(0, parent_item)
   464	
   465	        return folder_list
   466	
   467	    def _on_reload(self, *args) -> None:
   468	
   469	        # force the update of the current folder

   472	        return

Wait is there a way to optimize the loops further?

Looking again at the recurrence formula:

E[a][b] = 1 + p(a,b)*E[a][b+1] + (1-p(a,b))*E[a+1][b]

Looking at this, when processing for a, each row can be processed for b in reverse order.

Wait if we process the b's first for each a, perhaps we can represent B's as just an array, but it's not obvious.

Alternatively, maybe using 1D arrays instead of 2D can help. Since when processing from right to left, you can store the previous row's data.

Wait for a certain a, E[a][b] can be computed from E[a+1][b] (from the lower a row), and E[a][b+1], which has been processed earlier in the same loop.

Wait the formula relies on E[a][b+1], which for a fixed a is already computed since we process b from M down to 1.

So suppose for each a, you have a row array. 

Wait perhaps we can use a 1D array to save space and speed.

For example, let's have two arrays: previous_row and current_row.

Wait the idea is that for each a, the calculations depend on a's previous step (a+1) which was stored in previous.

Alternatively:

Initialize an array (of length M+2) for B's indices. 

Starting with a=N:

    for b from M downto 1:

        E_current[b] = 1 + p * E_current[b+1] + (1-p) * E_row_a_plus1[b]

    Here, E_row_a_plus1 is for a+1 (since we're handling a from high to low, a+1 would have been handled in the previous step)

Wait perhaps it's better to compute as:

Initialize a 1D array and process each a step.

Perhaps use a DP array with only a single row for each a. For example:

Let’s maintain the E for a and a-1 rows. 

Let's think of processing a in reverse order (from N downto 1). 

For each a, we can compute the new row based on the previous row (a+1), which has already been stored.

Thus, for a:

E_curr[b] = 1 + p[a][b]* (E_prev[b+1]) + (1-p[a][b])*( E[a+1][b])

Wait but a's previous row is a+1's b elements.

Wait, what I'm thinking:

Instead of a two-dimensional array, use two one-dimensional arrays: current and next. 

For a starting from N downto 1:

    For current 'a', we need E[a][b], which depends on E[a+1][b] (next row) and E[a][b+1] (current row's future value, if processed in proper order).

Wait:

If we process b from M down to 1, then for row a:

We can use a one-dimensional array that represents the current row's values for each b.

So, when we process b in reverse (M downto 1):

To compute E_current[b], we need E_prev_row (a+1) at b (i.e., the row for a+1), and also the current[b+1], which has been calculated already (as we process b in reverse).

For example:

The first step is a=N:

    current_row is initialized such that for a=N's processing, E[a+1][b] = E_prev_row[b] (which would be zero since a+1 >N).

Thus, when a=N:

    for b from M downto 1:

        E_current[b] = 1 + p * current_row[b+1 ] (which is from processing higher b first) + (1-p)*E_row_a_plus1[b] (which is E_prev_row[b] )

        However, since a+1 = N+1, the E_prev_row is zero.

    So, needs to set E_prev_row as all zeros.

Proceeding, here's the idea:

Let me structure it this way.

Initialize prev to be an array of size M+2 with all zeros. (This holds the E[a+1][...] values)

Then, iterate a from N down to 1:

    create a new current array (of size M+2).

    For b from M downto 1:

        p is prob[a-1][b-1]

        term1 = p * current[b+1]   (since when calculating b first, b+1 is already processed)

        term2 = (1-p) * prev[b]

        current[b] = 1.0 + term1 + term2

    # Save current row as prev for next loop (a-1)

    # after processing all b:

    prev = current  # but this is a copy?

Wait after processing a row for a, to move to a-1, the prev for that next a will be the current (current is current row for a's calculation).

Thus, for each a from N downto 1:

    for b in 1..M:

        compute current[b] using prev (a+1) and current's b+1.

for a in range(N, 0, -1):

    current = [0]*(M+2)

    for b in range(M,0,-1):

        p = prob[a-1][b-1]

        term1 = p * current[b+1]

        term2 = (1-p)* prev[b]

        current[b] = 1 + term1 + term2

    prev = current.copy()  # or a list assignment.

Thus, after each a step, prev becomes current for next iteration (a-1).

def main():
    import sys
    input = sys.stdin.read().split()
    idx = 0
    T = int(input[idx]); idx +=1
    for _ in range(T):
        N = int(input[idx]); idx +=1
        M = int(input[idx]); idx +=1
        prob = []
        for i in range(N):
            row = list(map(float, input[idx:idx+M]))
            prob.append(row)
            idx +=M
        # Initialize prev is the previous row (a+1), starting as all zeros for the next step (base is a = N+1)
        prev = [0.0]*(M+2)  # index from 0 to M+1
        for a in range(N, 0, -1):
            current = [0.0]*(M+2)
            for b in range(M, 0, -1):
                p = prob[a-1][b-1]
                term1 = p * current[b+1]  # since current is being filled from high b to low
                term2 = (1-p)* prev[b]  # prev (a+1's row)
                current[b] = 1.0 + term1 + term2
            # After processing all b's for this a, set prev to current for next a
            prev = current[:]   # Or alternate with another array if we want to reuse the same array?
            # But current is a new array each a step. So prev = current.copy() 

            # Alternatively:
            # To avoid creating new arrays each time, can swap arrays:
            # But need to think about the initializations.

            # prev, current = current, prev
            # But then need to reset current before each iteration.

            # The above is the simplest for code, though possible memory can be a bit worse as we are creating a new array each time, but for M=1e3, each array is 1e3 elements. Creating this every loop (1e3 times) for N=1e3 would require 1e3 * 1e3 steps, which is 1e6 elements in total, so 8MB memory each step. But this might be more manageable than 1e6 doubles in memory.

```python
import sys

def main():
    input = sys.stdin.read().split()
    idx = 0
    T = int(input[idx])
    idx += 1
    for _ in range(T):
        N = int(input[idx])
        M = int(input[idx+1])
        idx += 2
        prob = []
        for i in range(N):
            row = list(map(float, input[idx:idx+M]))
            prob.append(row)
            idx += M
        # Initialize prev to be 0-initialized array for a = N+1
        prev = [0.0] * (M + 2)  # indexes 0 to M+1, for b from 1 to M
        for a in range(N, 0, -1):
            current = [0.0] * (M + 2)
            for b in range(M, 0, -1):
                p = prob[a-1][b-1]
                term1 = p * current[b+1]
                term2 = (1.0 - p) * prev[b]
                current[b] = 1.0 + term1 + term2
            prev = current.copy()  # Copy the current to be prev for next iteration
        print("{0:.6f}".format(prev[1]))

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

### Explanation:
1. **Reading Input:** The input is read in one go and split into a list of strings for faster processing. The index variable keeps track of the current position in the input list.
2. **Matrix Initialization:** The `prob` matrix is read from the input.
3. **Dynamic Programming Setup:** We use two arrays `prev` and `current` to keep track of the previous and current values. This reduces the space complexity to O(M) instead of O(N*M).
4. **Backward Iteration:** We iterate over each player of team A from last to first (`a` from N down to 1).
5. **Calculating the Current State:** For each `a`, we iterate over all possible players of team B in reverse order (`b` from M down to 1). This allows us to compute the current value using previously calculated values (either from the current row ahead or the previous row).
6. **Transition Equation:** For each cell (a, b), we use the recurrence relation to determine the expected value.
7. **Updating Previous Array:** After processing all values for the current `a`, `prev` is updated to the current results to be used in the next iteration for `a-1`.

This approach efficiently computes the expected number of matches in O(N*M) time while using O(M) space, making it suitable for large values of N and M up to 1000.<#sep#>-yellow.svg

</think>To determine the number of real roots of the equation \(x^5 - 5x + 4 = 0\), we start by testing for potential rational roots using the Rational Root Theorem. We test \(x = 1\):

\[
1^5 - 5(1) + 4 = 1 - 5 + 4 = 0
\]

Thus, \(x = 1\) is a root. We can factor out \((x - 1)\) from the polynomial using synthetic division:

\[
\begin{array}{r|rrrrrr}
1 & 1 & 0 & 0 & 0 & -5 & 4 \\
  &   & 1 & 1 & 1 & 1 & -4 \\
\hline
  & 1 & 1 & 1 & 1 & -4 & 0 \\
\end{array}
\]

This gives us:

\[
x^5 - 5x + 4 = (x - 1)(x^4 + x^3 + x^2 + x - 4)
\]

Next, we test \(x = 1\) again on the quartic polynomial:

\[
1^4 + 1^3 + 1^2 + 1 - 4 = 1 + 1 + 1 + 1 - 4 = 0
\]

So, \(x = 1\) is a root again. We factor out \((x - 1)\) again using synthetic division:

\[
\begin{array}{r|rrrrr}
1 & 1 & 1 & 1 & 1 & -4 \\
  &   & 1 & 2 & 3 & 4 \\
\hline
  & 1 & 2 & 3 & 4 & 0 \\
\end{array}
\]

This gives us:

\[
x^4 + x^3 + x^2 + x - 4 = (x - 1)(x^3 + 2x^2 + 3x + 4)
\]

Thus, the original polynomial factors as:

\[
x^5 - 5x + 4 = (x - 1)^2(x^3 + 2x^2 + 3x + 4)
\]

Now, we analyze the cubic polynomial \(x^3 + 2x^2 + 3x + 4\). We test for rational roots using the Rational Root Theorem, but find no rational roots. To determine the number of real roots, we examine the derivative:

\[
f(x) = x^3 + 2x^2 + 3x + 4
\]
\[
f'(x) = 3x^2 + 4x + 3
\]

The discriminant of the quadratic \(3x^2 + 4x + 3\) is:

\[
\Delta = 4^2 - 4 \cdot 3 \cdot 3 = 16 - 36 = -20
\]

Since the discriminant is negative, \(f'(x)\) has no real roots and is always positive. Therefore, \(f(x)\) is strictly increasing. Evaluating \(f(x)\) at key points:

\[
f(-2) = (-2)^3 + 2(-2)^2 + 3(-2) + 4 = -8 + 8 - 6 + 4 = -2 < 0
\]
\[
f(-1) = (-1)^3 + 2(-1)^2 + 3(-1) + 4 = -1 + 2 - 3 + 4 = 2 > 0
\]

Since \(f(x)\) is strictly increasing and changes sign between \(-2\) and \(-1\), it has exactly one real root in this interval.

Thus, the original polynomial \(x^5 - 5x + 4\) has:
- A double root at \(x = 1\)
- One real root from the cubic polynomial between \(-2\) and \(-1\)

Therefore, the equation \(x^5 - 5x + 4 = 0\) has \(\boxed{2}\) distinct real roots.<#sep#>.

   360	    def _on_file_select(self, change: dict) -> Self:
   361	        """
   362	        Dispatch the behavior between file selection and folder change.
   363	        """
   364	        if not change["new"]:
   365	            return self
   366	
   367	        new_value = Path(change["new"])
   368	
   369	        if new_value.is_dir():
   370	            self.folder = new_value
   371	            self._change_folder()
   372	
   373	        elif new_value.is_file():
   374	            self.file = str(new_value)
   375	
   376	        return self

   378	    @sd.switch("indeterminate", on_widgets=["loading"])
   379	    def _change_folder(self) -> None:
   380	        """
   381	        Change the target folder.
   382	        """
   383	        # get the items
   384	        items = self._get_items()
   385	
   386	        # reset files
   387	        # this is reseting the scroll to top without using js scripts
   388	        self.file_list.children[0].children = []
   389	
   390	        # set the new files
   391	        self.file_list.children[0].children = items
   392	
   393	        return
   394	
   395	    def _get_items(self) -> List[v.ListItem]:
   396	        """
   397	        Create the list of items inside the folder.
   398	
   399	        Returns:
   400	            list of items inside the selected folder
   401	        """
   402	        folder = self.folder
   403	
   404	        list_dir = [el for el in folder.glob("*/") if not el.name.startswith(".")]
   405	
   406	        if self.extentions:
   407	            list_dir = [
   408	                el for el in list_dir if el.is_dir() or el.suffix in self.extentions
   409	            ]
   410	
   411	        folder_list = []
   412	        file_list = []
   413	
   414	        for el in list_dir:
   415	
   416	            if el.is_dir():
   417	                icon = self.ICON_STYLE[""]["icon"]
   418	                color = self.ICON_STYLE[""]["color"][v.theme.dark]
   419	            elif el.suffix in self.ICON_STYLE.keys():
   420	                icon = self.ICON_STYLE[el.suffix]["icon"]
   421	                color = self.ICON_STYLE[el.suffix]["color"][v.theme.dark]
   422	            else:
   423	                icon = self.ICON_STYLE["DEFAULT"]["icon"]
   424	                color = self.ICON_STYLE["DEFAULT"]["color"][v.theme.dark]
   425	
   426	            children = [
   427	                v.ListItemAction(children=[v.Icon(color=color, children=[icon])]),
   428	                v.ListItemContent(
   429	                    children=[v.ListItemTitle(children=[el.stem + el.suffix])]
   430	                ),
   431	            ]
   432	
   433	            if el.is_dir():
   434	                folder_list.append(v.ListItem(value=str(el), children=children))
   435	            else:
   436	                file_size = su.get_file_size(el)
   437	                children.append(
   438	                    v.ListItemActionText(class_="ml-1", children=[file_size])
   439	                )
   440	                file_list.append(v.ListItem(value=str(el), children=children))
   441	
   442	        folder_list = humansorted(folder_list, key=lambda x: x.value)
   443	        file_list = humansorted(file_list, key=lambda x: x.value)
   444	
   445	        parent_item = v.ListItem(
   446	            value=str(folder.parent),
   447	            children=[
   448	                v.ListItemAction(

851:    increm: t<#sep#> This is equivalent to saying that the graph does not contain an independent set of size 4. Therefore, any graph that doesn't have an independent set of size 4 is allowed. The complete graph obviously satisfies this, as there are no independent sets. However, there's a result in extremal graph theory called the Moon-Moser theorem, which states that the maximum number of triangles in a graph of order n with no independent set of size r+1. In our case, r=3, so no independent set of size 4. For n=9, the maximum number of triangles is given by the complete 3-partite graph with partitions of size 3, which is K3,3,3 with 27 triangles. So, this might be the answer.

Wait, but why can't we have more triangles? Because the Moon-Moser theorem states that the maximum number of triangles in an n-vertex graph with no independent set of size r+1 is achieved by the complete r-partite graph with partitions as equal as possible. The actual conditions for a segment being lit is that it is not obscured by segments to its right. A segment is lit along its full length if all parts of itare not shadowed by any rightward segments.

Thus, a segment AB is lit if there is no segment further to the right  (i.e., with endpoint at higher x) that has a steeper slope from B.

The way to compute this is to iterate from the end and keep track of the minimal angle or maximal slope.

Let me try the following algorithm approach:

We can process the segments from right to left, keeping track of the steepest\最 steep slope encountered so far.

The lit segments are those where their slope is more steep than the"current steepest slope from the sunset View"。 

具体:

- 开始, the final segment has its slope, and it is lit completely.

- 维护 the current ‘critical slope’ that any new segment must have a slope ≥ critical slope to be lit.

</think>To solve the problem, we need to find the maximum number of triangles (groups of three people who all know each other) in a graph of 9 vertices where every group of 4 vertices contains at least one edge. This condition ensures the graph has no independent set of size 4, which implies its complement has no clique of size 4.

1. **Understanding the Condition:**
   - The condition "for every group of 4 people, at least 2 of them know each other" means the graph does not contain an independent set of size 4.
   - Equivalently, the complement graph does not contain a clique of size 4.

2. **Applying Turán's Theorem:**
   - According to Turán's theorem, the maximum number of edges in a graph with \( n \) vertices that does not contain a \( K_4 \) (a clique of size 4) is achieved by the Turán graph \( T(n, 3) \).
   - For \( n = 9 \), the Turán graph \( T(9, 3) \) is a complete 3-partite graph with each partition having 3 vertices.

3. **Structure of the Complement Graph:**
   - The complement graph \( G' \) is \( T(9, 3) \), which is a complete 3-partite graph with partitions \( A, B, \) and \( C \), each of size 3.
   - In \( G' \), there are no edges within each partition, but there are edges between different partitions.

4. **Structure of the Original Graph \( G \):**
   - The original graph \( G \) is the complement of \( G' \).
   - Therefore, \( G \) has edges within each partition (since \( G' \) has no edges within each partition), and no edges between different partitions (since \( G' \) has edges between different partitions).

5. **Counting Triangles in \( G \):**
   - Each partition in \( G \) forms a complete subgraph (clique) of size 3, which is a triangle.
   - Since there are three such partitions, each contributing one triangle, the total number of triangles in \( G \) is \( 3 \).

6. **Maximizing Triangles:**
   - To maximize the number of triangles while ensuring the condition is met, we consider the complete tripartite graph \( K_{3,3,3} \).
   - In \( K_{3,3,3} \), each triangle involves one vertex from each of the three partitions.
   - The number of such triangles is \( 3 \times 3 \times 3 = 27 \).

7. **Conclusion:**
   - The maximum number of triangles \( f \) in the graph \( G \) is \(\boxed{27}\).<#sep#>eeper slope from B.

当 reverse processing, this segments is considered 从 B到 A, so the 'direction' is leftwards. the slope would be deltaY / deltaX = (y_A - y_B) / (x_A - x_B): which is the negative of the slope when going from A to B.

这样, the slope的 sign is such that 如果 the segment is going 下坡 from the sun's direction (right到 left), it has a negative slope 如果 <0, which may indicate that it is 下 slope只要 compared to the egged slopes.

可能 the critical slope is the minimum slope_required, such that any segment must have a slope >= the之前 right side's minimum slope.

 When this is the case, the current segment is lit and its contITIBUT starts the new/current minimal slope, 否则, it's not lit.

但 this is getting too vague.

Maybe we can think in terms of 这 condition:

每个 segment is lit if its slope (from right to left) is more_than_ordered to all the previous 得 to its right. 

Thus the critical slope is 维护 the minimal slope 遇到 so far, and any segment whose slope is更大的 than 或等于 this will be lit and then new minimal is updated.

 thus the algo:

 초기화:

 minimal_slope = infinity. 

 total=0.

 previous_point =<#sep#> script that reproduces the located issue.
