   <#sep#>In Pascal's Triangle, each entry is the sum of the two entries above it. In which row of Pascal's Triangle do three consecutive entries occur that are in the ratio \(3:4:5\)?

<think>
Okay, so I need to find the row in Pascal's Triangle where there are three consecutive entries in the ratio 3:4:5. Hmm, let me start by recalling that in Pascal's Triangle, each row n has entries corresponding to the binomial coefficients C(n, 0), C(n, 1), ..., C(n, n). The problem is asking for three consecutive entries in some row n, say C(n, k-1), C(n, k), and C(n, k+1), such that their ratios are 3:4:5. 

First, let me set up the ratios. If the three consecutive entries are in the ratio 3:4:5, then:

C(n, k) / C(n, k-1) = 4/3

and

C(n, k+1) / C(n, k) = 5/4.

Alternatively, maybe I can write the equations as:

C(n, k) = (4/3) * C(n, k-1)

and

C(n, k+1) = (5/4) * C(n, k)

That's probably a better way to handle it. Now, I know that in Pascal's Triangle, each entry is the sum of the two above it, but also, the ratio between consecutive entries can be expressed using the combination formula. Let's recall that C(n, k) = n! / (k!(n - k)!).

So, the ratio C(n, k) / C(n, k-1) can be simplified. Let me compute that:

C(n, k) / C(n, k-1) = [n! / (k!(n - k)!)] / [n! / ((k-1)!(n - k + 1)!)] 

Simplify that:

= [(k-1)!(n - k + 1)!] / [k!(n - k)!]

= [(n - k + 1)/k]

So, the ratio C(n, k)/C(n, k-1) = (n - k + 1)/k

Similarly, the ratio C(n, k+1)/C(n, k) = (n - k)/(k + 1)

Given the problem, these ratios should be 4/3 and 5/4 respectively.

So, setting up the equations:

(n - k + 1)/k = 4/3  ...(1)

(n - k)/(k + 1) = 5/4  ...(2)

Now, I need to solve these two equations for n and k.

Let me first solve equation (1) for n. Let's rearrange equation (1):

(n - k + 1) = (4/3)k

So, n - k + 1 = (4/3)k

Bring all terms to left-hand side:

n + 1 = (4/3)k + k

n + 1 = (7/3)k

Therefore, n = (7/3)k - 1  ...(a)

Similarly, take equation (2):

(n - k) = (5/4)(k + 1)

So, n - k = (5/4)(k + 1)

Let me express n from here:

n = (5/4)(k + 1) + k

= (5/4)k + 5/4 + k

= (5/4 + 4/4)k + 5/4

= (9/4)k + 5/4 ...(b)

Now, from equations (a) and (b):

From (a): n = (7/3)k - 1

From (b): n = (9/4)k + 5/4

Set them equal:

(7/3)k - 1 = (9/4)k + 5/4

Multiply both sides by 12 to eliminate denominators:

12*(7/3)k - 12*1 = 12*(9/4)k + 12*(5/4)

Simplify:

4*7k - 12 = 3*9k + 3*5

28k - 12 = 27k + 15

Subtract 27k from both sides:

k - 12 = 15

Add 12 to both sides:

k = 27

Now, plug k = 27 into equation (a) to find n:

n = (7/3)*27 - 1

7/3 * 27 = 7 * 9 = 63

So, n = 63 - 1 = 62

Alternatively, check with equation (b):

n = (9/4)*27 + 5/4

Calculate 9/4 *27:

27 divided by 4 is 6.75, multiplied by 9: 60.75

5/4 is 1.25

So, 60.75 + 1.25 = 62. So, n=62. Correct.

So, n=62, k=27.

Therefore, the row is row 62.

The Gameboy stores tiles as 2 bit-per-pixel 8x8 images, thus 16 bytes. Every two bytes is a complete row with all of the Low-bits of each pixel in the first byte, and all of the High-bits of each pixel in the second.
Input
Input will be exactly 16 bytes, received through Standard IO  in one of the following forms:

Array of bytes or strings
16 byte string

Per the Standard IO, these may be in a language convenient form (Deliminated string, on the stack, etc.)
Output
An image, Rendered or Saved, of the Gameboy Tile.
Scale and Aspect Ratio of each pixel is not fixed.
Each 2 bit colour of the Pallet may be anything so long as the Manhattan Distance of the RGB256 representation is atleast 48. Eg. #FFFFFF, #AAAAAA, #555555, #000000. Traditionally, although not a requirement, 00 is the lightest colour, and 11 is the darkest.
Examples
[FF, 00, 7E, FF, 85, 81, 89, 83, 93, 85, A5, 8B, C9, 97, 7E, FF]

[7C, 7C, 00, C6, C6, 00, 00, FE, C6, C6, 00, C6, C6, 00, 00, 00]

Final Notes

Standard Loopholes apply
An online demo, and more in-depth explanation of the implementation can be found HERE
This is code-golf, so fewest bytes wins! (The Gameboy only had 65536 bytes of Address Space, after all!)
Have Fun!

We had:

From equation (1):

(n -k +1)/k = 4/3

Which is (n +1 -k)/k =4/3

Cross multiply: 3(n +1 -k) =4k

3n +3 -3k =4k

3n +3=7k

Similarly, equation (2):

(n -k)/(k +1)=5/4

Cross multiply:4(n -k)=5(k +1)

4n -4k=5k +5

4n=9k +5

So, now we have two equations:

3n +3=7k ...(1a)

4n=9k +5 ...(2a)

Now, from (1a): 3n=7k -3

Therefore, n=(7k -3)/3

Substitute into (2a):

4*(7k -3)/3 =9k +5

Multiply both sides by 3:

4*(7k -3)=27k +15

28k -12=27k +15

28k -27k=15+12

k=27

Then n=(7*27 -3)/3= (189 -3)/3=186/3=62. Correct.

So yes, k=27 and n=62. Therefore, the row is 62.

Therefore, the answer is row 62.

**Final Answer**
The three consecutive entries in the ratio \(3:4:5\) occur in row \boxed{62} of Pascal's Triangle.
</think>To find the row in Pascal's Triangle where three consecutive entries are in the ratio \(3:4:5\), we start by using the binomial coefficients \(C(n, k-1)\), \(C(n, k)\), and \(C(n, k+1)\). These coefficients must satisfy the ratios:

\[
\frac{C(n, k)}{C(n, k-1)} = \frac{4}{3} \quad \text{and} \quad \frac{C(n, k+1)}{C(n, k)} = \frac{5}{4}
\]

Using the property of binomial coefficients, the ratios can be expressed as:

\[
\frac{C(n, k)}{C(n, k-1)} = \frac{n - k + 1}{k} \quad \text{and} \quad \frac{C(n, k+1)}{C(n, k)} = \frac{n - k}{k + 1}
\]

Setting up the equations based on the given ratios:

1. \(\frac{n - k + 1}{k} = \frac{4}{3}\)
2. \(\frac{n - k}{k + 1} = \frac{5}{4}\)

Solving the first equation for \(n\):

\[
n - k + 1 = \frac{4}{3}k \implies n + 1 = \frac{7}{3}k \implies n = \frac{7}{3}k - 1
\]

Solving the second equation for \(n\):

\[
n - k = \frac{5}{4}(k + 1) \implies n = \frac{9}{4}k + \frac{5}{4}
\]

Equating the two expressions for \(n\):

\[
\frac{7}{3}k - 1 = \frac{9}{4}k + \frac{5}{4}
\]

Multiplying through by 12 to clear the denominators:

\[
28k - 12 = 27k + 15 \implies k = 27
\]

Substituting \(k = 27\) back into the expression for \(n\):

\[
n = \frac{7}{3} \cdot 27 - 1 = 63 - 1 = 62
\]

To verify, we check the ratios:

\[
\frac{C(62, 27)}{C(62, 26)} = \frac{62 - 27 + 1}{27} = \frac{36}{27} = \frac{4}{3}
\]

\[
\frac{C(62, 28)}{C(62, 27)} = \frac{62 - 27}{27 + 1} = \frac{35}{28} = \frac{5}{4}
\]

Both ratios hold, confirming that the three consecutive entries in row 62 of Pascal's Triangle are in the ratio \(3:4:5\).

Therefore, the row in Pascal's Triangle where three consecutive entries are in the ratio \(3:4:5\) is:

(b c)/(b + 2c) - 0 = (b c)/(b + 2c)

Thus, slope m_AD = [ -b(b + c)/(b + 2c) ] / [ b c/(b + 2c) ] = [ -b(b + c) ] / (b c) ) = - (b + c)/c

So the slope of AD is - (b + c)/c.

def f(bz): # accepts list of 16 bytes (integers 0-255)

  im = Image.new('RGB', (8,8))

  pix = im.load()

  for i in range(8): # row index 0-7

    rlow = bz[2*i]

    rhigh = bz[2*i+1]

    for j in 0..7 (for each pixel in row):

      l_bit = (rlow >> j) &1 

      h_bit= (rhigh >>j ) &1

      c_val= (h_bit <<1 ) | l_bit

      color = ( (3-c_val)*85, (3-c_val)*85, (3-c_val)*85 ) 

      # because 0 → 3*85 =255, 1: 255-85,... 

      # so for 0: (255, ... ) which is white; 3 is 0,0,0.

      # The colors are thus exactly the ones in the example: the four colors spaced by 85 per component.

      # so the Manhattan distance between 0 (255,255,255) and 1 (170, etc) is 85*3=255, which is way over the 48 requirement.

      pix[j,i] = color # assuming the order (x,y): x= j, y= i.

      # if the image has rows starting from top to bottom, that's correct.

Wait in code golf, sometimes people use functions that generate the colors and return a list of pixels which can be saved as an image. But the problem requires the image to be "rendered or saved". So the code must actually produce it, but the method may vary.

Perhaps code that outputs an image in PPM P3 format. PPM is a simple format: header P3, size 8x8, max 255, then 8x8 array of RGB triplets in ASCII.

The headers would be P3\n8 8\n255\n\n and then each row as numbers from 0-255, space-separated. This way, generating a plaintext output could be feasible, which is an "image".

So generating PPM/P3 format would allow to code this in a short way, and then the user would have to open that file, but perhaps the problem allows that.

So P3 with the colors (three numbers per pixel, space-separated, each 0-255).

Thus the steps would be:

- for each of the 8 rows (i in 0-7):

   low = bw[2*i]

   high = bw[2*i+1]

   for j in 0-7 pixels:

      compute color as 255 - (value*85) for all RGB components.

      Where value is the 2bit value (0-3), so:

        value = ( ( (high >>j) &1 ) <<1 ) | ( (low >>j) &1 )

      Then, assuming (as in earlier example):

       color = ( (3-value)*85 ) for R G B.

So for value 0 → 255, 1=170, etc.

Thus the RGB triple would be ( (3 - v)*85, same, same )

Thus, the PPM code would look something like: write header then for each row, output 8 triples for the line.

Alternatively, using coordinates, the area of triangle AEL can be calculated using the determinant formula:

Area = (1/2) | x_A(y_E - y_L) + x_E(y_L - y_A) + x_L(y_A - y_E) |

Plugging in the coordinates:

x_A = 0, y_A = b

x_E = x_L = (b c)/(b + c) = x * ( (b + c)/b c ) ) Wait, no. Wait, x_E = x_L = (b c)/(b + c). Since EL = x, which is (b c)/(b + c), so (b c)/(b + c) = x. So x_E = x_L = x. Wait, no, x_E and x_L are both (b c)/(b + c), which is equal to x. So, substituting x_E = x, x_L = x, y_E = 0, y_L = x, y_A = b.

Then:

Area = (1/2) | 0*(0 - x) + x*(x - b) + x*(b - 0) | = (1/2) | 0 + x(x - b) + x b | = (1/2) | x² - b x + b x | = (1/2) | x² | = (1/2)x²

Therefore, the area is x²/2.

Wow, so despite the complex setup with angle bisectors and medians intersecting, the area ends up being simply half of x squared. That seems elegant. Let me just check if all the steps make sense and there's no miscalculation.

We started by assigning coordinates, found the coordinates of points L, D, E through the angle bisector theorem and solving the equations of the lines. Then, once we had all the points, we noticed that EL is vertical and equal to x, then computed the area accordingly, which gave x²/2. I think this is correct. So the answer should be \boxed{\dfrac{x^2}{2}}.
</think>To find the area of triangle \(AEL\) in a right-angled triangle \(ABC\) with a right angle at \(B\), given that \(EL = x\), we proceed as follows:

1. **Coordinate System Setup**:
   - Place \(B\) at \((0, 0)\), \(A\) at \((0, b)\), and \(C\) at \((c, 0)\).

2. **Finding Coordinates of \(L\)**:
   - The angle bisector \(BL\) divides \(AC\) in the ratio \(AB:BC = b:c\).
   - Using the section formula, the coordinates of \(L\) are:
     \[
     L = \left( \frac{bc}{b+c}, \frac{bc}{b+c} \right)
     \]

3. **Equation of Line \(BL\)**:
   - The line \(BL\) passes through \((0,0)\) and \(\left( \frac{bc}{b+c}, \frac{bc}{b+c} \right)\), so its equation is \(y = x\).

4. **Equation of Median \(CM\)**:
   - The median \(CM\) connects \(C(c,0)\) to the midpoint \(M\) of \(AB\), which is \((0, \frac{b}{2})\).
   - The slope of \(CM\) is \(-\frac{b}{2c}\), and its equation is:
     \[
     y = -\frac{b}{2c}(x - c)
     \]

5. **Finding Intersection Point \(D\)**:
   - Substitute \(y = x\) into the equation of \(CM\):
     \[
     x = -\frac{b}{2c}(x - c)
     \]
     \[
     2cx = -b(x - c)
     \]
     \[
     2cx = -bx + bc
     \]
     \[
     (2c + b)x = bc
     \]
     \[
     x = \frac{bc}{b + 2c}
     \]
     Thus, the coordinates of \(D\) are:
     \[
     D = \left( \frac{bc}{b + 2c}, \frac{bc}{b + 2c} \right)
     \]

6. **Equation of Line \(AD\)**:
   - The line \(AD\) passes through \(A(0, b)\) and \(D\left( \frac{bc}{b + 2c}, \frac{bc}{b + 2c} \right)\).
   - The slope of \(AD\) is:
     \[
     \text{slope} = \frac{\frac{bc}{b + 2c} - b}{\frac{bc}{b + 2c} - 0} = \frac{\frac{bc - b(b + 2c)}{b + 2c}}{\frac{bc}{b + 2c}} = \frac{-b(b + c)}{bc} = -\frac{b + c}{c}
     \]
   - The equation of \(AD\) is:
     \[
     y - b = -\frac{b + c}{c}x
     \]
     \[
     y = -\frac{b + c}{c}x + b
     \]

7. **Finding Intersection Point \(E\)**:
   - Set \(y = 0\) in the equation of \(AD\):
     \[
     0 = -\frac{b + c}{c}x + b
     \]
     \[
     \frac{b + c}{c}x = b
     \]
     \[
     x = \frac{bc}{b + c}
     \]
     Thus, the coordinates of \(E\) are:
     \[
     E = \left( \frac{bc}{b + c}, 0 \right)
     \]

8. **Length \(EL\)**:
   - The coordinates of \(E\) and \(L\) are \(\left( \frac{bc}{b + c}, 0 \right)\) and \(\left( \frac{bc}{b + c}, \frac{bc}{b + c} \right)\), respectively.
   - The vertical distance \(EL\) is:
     \[
     EL = \frac{bc}{b + c} = x
     \]

9. **Area of Triangle \(AEL\)**:
   - The base \(EL = x\) and the height is the horizontal distance from \(A\) to the line \(x = \frac{bc}{b + c}\), which is also \(x\).
   - The area of triangle \(AEL\) is:
     \[
     \text{Area} = \frac{1}{2} \times \text{base} \times \text{height} = \frac{1}{2} \times x \times x = \frac{x^2}{2}
     \]

Thus, the area of triangle \(AEL\) is \(\boxed{\frac{x^2}{2}}\).<#sep#>255, 1=170, etc.

Then for j from0 to7:

计算:

h>>j <<1 → since h=0, all bits are 0 → so h is zero, thus any j:

h>>j =0 → left <<1 →0.

Add the l's bit.

so for each j:

高为零, so:

value是0的左移一位 →0.再加 the low bit of l (the j bit of 0xff which is 1 →因为 0xff的每个bit 是1.

所以v =0+1=1.

3-1 =2 → value是: 2 *85 →170.

So each of the 8 x的 elements is 170.

thus, the first row would have all 170, repeated thrice 8 times → each pixel is 170 in R, G, B.

This is correct.

Second row:

l=0x7e, h=0XFF → 255 for h.

So the first j=0:

h 这的j=0 bit.

h is 0xff (binary 8 ones), each bit is 1 → h>>0 →255 → bitwise>>的J=0 → 255 →如, 

h>>j是255（>>0 means no shift.

but这时 (h>>j)>>=的值是255?

But >>j for j=0 is no shift → value的 high contribution is:

high_bit是 (h的第j位 → the bit here is 1.

(the high-bit --> so when j=0, the high_bit is 1 → 高的比特值是1被左移一位 becomes 2.

加上 the low bit的 bit j of low of 0x7e → (0x7e) >>0的 which is 0x7e的最后一位 is zero → 0.

So v = 2+0 =2.

 thus, 3-2 =1 → 85 → contributes to the d为85.

Next, for j=1:

 high_bit is 1 → high contribution is 2, and the low_bit of L is 1 (since 0x7e的bit1 is 1).

因此 v = 2 +1 = 3 → 3-3 =0 →0 → the fourth color.

Phase 1. READING: read the problem and reword it in clearer terms
   1.1 If there are code or config snippets. Express in words any best practices or conventions in them.
   1.2 Hightlight message errors, method names, variables, file names, stack traces, and technical details.
   1.3 Explain the problem in clear terms.
   1.4 Enumerate the steps to reproduce the problem.
   1.5 Hightlight any best practices to take into account when testing and fixing the issue

Phase 2. RUNNING: install and run the tests on the repository
   2.1 Follow the readme
   2.2 Install the environment and anything needed
   2.2 Iterate and figure out how to run the tests

Phase 3. EXPLORATION: find the files that are related to the problem and possible solutions
   3.1 Use `grep` to search for relevant methods, classes, keywords and error messages.
   3.2 Identify all files related to the problem statement.
   3.3 Propose the methods and files to fix the issue and explain why.
   3.4 From the possible file locations, select the most likely location to fix the issue.

Phase 4. TEST CREATION: before implementing any fix, create a script to reproduce and verify the issue.
   4.1 Look at existing test files in the repository to understand the test format/structure.
   4.2 Create a minimal reproduction script that reproduces the located issue.
   4.3 Run the reproduction script to confirm you are reproducing the issue.
   4.4 Adjust the reproduction script as necessary.

Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS: state clearly the problem and how to fix it
   5.1 State clearly what the problem is.
   5.2 State clearly where the problem is located.
   5.3 State clearly how the test reproduces the issue.
   5.4 State clearly the best practices to take into account in the fix.
   5.5 State clearly how to fix the problem.

Phase
